# Finishing the proof: instructions for workers

Written by the target lead on 2026-10-31. `GOAL.md` holds the target and the definition of non-trivial, and it wins over this file and over the charters. The charters' interface wording, UV-EXT, is superseded. This is research-stage working state, and nothing here grants status.

## Where it stands

**T0 chain, 2026-11-03 evening:** every clause of T0 is now written as an implication (rows 161-201): RP and symmetry at every mirror, compactness and the Hilbert space (O1, O4), growth (E0'), the spectral cone (O6), O(4) by UI-PD-AK-TU-AC (O7), clustering and components (O8, O9), the Wightman theory (O11). They rest on D1 (the hard core's source bounds), TU10, E1's cutoff sequence, and non-triviality (O10, via F2). Most of these notes are Codex's and wait for Claude's check (row 187). The hard core's open items: B4 and 1.6 (via (20) <= (TI), (ER7), (BX)), (LOC), and rank 7's repair.

**Target changed 2026-11-03 (owner):** T0, the theorem OpenAI claims, is now the target: the full Osterwalder-Schrader axioms with O(4), growth and clustering (GOAL.md). The Wightman reconstruction is part of T0 (owner, same day), rebuilt here so the proof runs top to bottom, with OS II and YM3_I cited for the method. T1 is a milestone on the way. Ranks 11, 12 and 17-20 are back on the path.

**Existence is not proved.** As of 2026-11-03:

## What finishing means

1. The hard core is closed, by our route, the outline's, or a mix as `library/route-decisions.md` records, with every statement it reads verified here, and it delivers an interface statement the upper chain reads as written.
2. The upper chain's conditional theorems lose their conditions.
3. NT1 and NT2 hold, as `parts/witness.md` states them.
4. One clean document holds the whole chain, followed by a line-by-line independent review of it, then the owner's approval of the exact text. Balaban and Dimock are credited by name, and so are Ethan Sussman and Levent Alpoge (with Claude and Codex as authors of their text) for every step, idea or construction taken from their repository, and Grok for anything taken from its pull requests here, in each note that uses it, in the final document and in any public post. Until then there is no public claim.

**Rarity, the next question (`rarity-fresh`).** The consumers need a full-law bound on marked events under class parents, with product form for compatible marks and control through rough intermediate parents. Known: raw fine energy is extensive on typical fields (Lemma TYP), so a raw fine-energy trigger cannot be rare; `heavy-wall` shows the stage's layer selection has no output beside a heavy wall; chart concentration does not bound the complement; centre-plane chessboard fails on the class branch (`one-window` Section 3); a uniform local gain fails. (HW) has no refutation. Propose and attack one complete route to a full-mass producer that the consumers can read, and say which consumers change. You may change the state or a statement if you say what breaks. Do not narrow to a smaller inequality as your output: end by naming the first step of your route that fails, or carry it through.




## How we work

0. **Port the complete account (owner, 2026-11-05).** Read GOAL.md's method section and `library/port-map.md` first. Port what Sussman and Alpoge prove; put proof lanes only on their open statements on the T0 chain and on the T0 clauses they do not assert. Each FINISH row names the CA label and its status.

0. **Is it worth doing? Ask before every lane, check, edit or message, lead included.** It must name the open item on the critical path that it closes or kills (an obligation in `GOAL.md`, a row of `library/sa-ledger.md`'s top twenty, or a position in `library/route-decisions.md`), and what we would do differently if it succeeds or fails. No named item, or no change either way: do not do it. Each queue row opens with "Moves:" and that item. Each lead update lists what moved; work that moved nothing is named as waste, so it is not repeated.
1. **Literature first.** Each route or proof step starts from one wide search (Balaban, Dimock, the Sussman-Alpoge repository at `31a47c543775`, arXiv, older constructive field theory), recorded once in `library/lit-<topic>.md` and reused. A smaller lane reads those records and does a short targeted search.
2. **Build on published work; verify what the proof uses.** No firewall. Use any published result at once, labelled `unchecked`, credited by name (Balaban, Dimock, Sussman and Alpoge with Claude and Codex as authors of their text, Grok for its PRs #10133 to #10135). Before it counts as closed, it is re-derived in `library/` and checked by another provider. `library/sa-ledger.md` lists what the outline's Theorem 0 reads, with the twenty to verify first.
3. **Choose routes on evidence.** Ours and the outline's are both candidates, part by part. `library/route-decisions.md` records the choice, the reason and what would reverse it. Keep both live only while the evidence is open; budget follows evidence.
4. **Only closures count.** A lane ends with a proof, every step written, or an explicit counterexample. Try a counterexample first only when unsure. A smaller statement is attacked in the same session; after two reductions with no closure, stop and report to the lead. Checks redo the proof and report WRONG with the failure, or confirm it. Notes are short.
5. **Skeleton first, then fast (owner, 2026-11-03: "get the proof right and get it fast, and skelleton it first maybe"; "dont spend time on adverse readings").** `library/t0-skeleton.md` lists every statement T0 needs, in order, with its inputs and its state: proved and checked, proved and not yet checked, open, or cited only. Each lane fills one open node and updates its line. Each proof gets one check: the other model reads it line by line once. No hostile-referee passes, no reviews of reviews, no extra readings for scope or wording. When the next lane reads a result anyway, it does the check in its first section, not in a separate lane. Read the same step in their QED3 and YM3 manuscripts first. Freeze what passes; reopen only on WRONG or a changed input. Keep every machine on the critical path. Hardening (hostile readings, stress tests, robustness passes) waits until the whole proof exists (owner, 2026-11-03: "once we have the proofs we can do the reinforcement thing"); it then runs on the clean document of "What finishing means" item 4.
6. **Our proof first; help Sussman-Alpoge where it has real value (owner, 2026-11-03: "I dont view them as a rival, we should help where it makes sense to do so, and continue otherwise. Just hold a high bar for contributing").** Workers never post outside this repository. The lead may offer a GitHub issue to the Sussman-Alpoge repository only for a result checked line by line by the other model that they would value: an actual error in their text with the explicit failure, or a proof of something their outline leaves open or assumes. Not gaps in wording, missing references, notation or bookkeeping. **Nothing is posted without the owner's approval of the exact text** (owner, 2026-11-04, after issue #8: "we had ONE rule - be right"). The earlier authorization to post without asking is withdrawn by the lead. A candidate reaches the owner only after five checks, in order (owner, same day: "I would like you to be REALLY sure its value before we do it to offset our noise"): (1) correct: checked line by line by the other model, first failing step none; (2) new: the other model, reading every outline passage that defines or proves the object, answers in writing that the outline does not prove it, and the lead reads those passages too; (3) about their text: a correction must fail their statement as written, not a reading or representative we chose; (4) valuable to them: the lead writes, from their side, what it changes in their proof; (5) the owner approves the exact text. One well-checked contribution beats several. Before posting, the lead reads every outline section that defines or proves the claimed object (grep its tags and names), and the other model answers one question in writing: does the outline already prove this, anywhere? (Issue #8, 2026-11-04, claimed the outline did not show a pivot value it constructs in (QC22)-(QC24); corrected and closed within the hour.) No lane works on their statements unless our chain reads them.
7. **Say who checked what, in plain words (owner, 2026-11-03).** Write "checked line by line by Codex (or Claude), first failing step none" or "WRONG at <step>", and "proved by Claude, not yet checked by the other model". Never write "gated" or "ungated". A check is one reading by one other model: not an audit and not human review, and a checked result is still an implication from its named inputs. Check reports still go in `gates/`.
9. **Check their proof first; rebuild only what they do not prove (owner, 2026-11-04: "how is it that we are working on proofs that they already have and then being confused about them having them").** Before proving anything, a lane finds where the outline treats the object (grep its names, tags and equation labels, and read every passage that defines or proves it) and quotes it, classifying it as proved, sketched or assumed. If the outline proves it in detail, the lane takes it as a cited input and moves on; its line-by-line check waits for the hardening pass on the final document (owner, same day: "how would that add value / or push forward"). Lanes are spent only where the outline sketches, assumes or gets something wrong, and on checking our own new text. A note may say "the outline assumes X" or "does not show X" only with the quoted passages that build X. Each Result says which kind it is: check of their proof, proof of a step they sketch or assume, or correction of their text.
10. **The lead checks the outline before launching (owner, 2026-11-04: "before you kick off any exploration please make sure alpoge doesnt already have the proof and we are just doing pointless work ok?").** Before any proof lane starts, the lead (not only the lane) looks the object up in `library/sa-index/` (row 244: grep the tag or name across its chunk files and read the neighbouring rows), reads the passages that define, prove or assume it, and writes an **Outline (lead):** line in the row: the lines read, a short quote, and proved / sketched / assumed. If the outline proves it in detail, the row is not launched; the object becomes a cited input. Checks of our own new text need no such line.
11. **Proof lanes run to closure (lead, 2026-11-04, applying the hard-core standard).** Rows 256-261 ran 8-19 minutes at max effort and each stopped at its first open input, because the prompt said "name the first open input". A proof lane now runs in rounds (`deep_lane.sh`, up to 3 rounds of 2 h): each round reads the note's own Result and proves the named input, then the next. It stops only at a full proof, a counterexample, or a step attacked three distinct ways, stated as one precise lemma with why each way failed. A missing paper is never a stopping point; we rebuild it (owner: "why cant we just design and prove that balaban work ourselves").
8. **Write about 80% of the way to ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English (owner, 2026-11-03, after Karpathy's 2026-10-02 post).** Short sentences, about 20 words at most, one topic each, active voice, one meaning per word and the same term every time, no noun stacks longer than three words. Claim first, then evidence, then what it does not show. Math notation is exempt. This binds issue drafts, library notes and check reports. Notes are as short as the proof allows.

## Queue

Take the first item that is not taken, unless the lead has named one for you. An item is taken if a file matching its output exists with a live lease, or a finished report. Pin the statement at the branch head and put the commit in the file name. A worker on the Mac mini takes these items in order, one at a time, so check the branch before starting one.

Past rows are in `QUEUE_DONE.md`.

## Joining

Use a fresh session on Opus 5.5 at the item's effort. Do not resume the sessions of the running pushes.

```bash
python3 scripts/lab_ops.py worktree-add ym4d-<item> --target claude/ym4d-hard-route --path .claude/science/physics-loops/ym4d-hard-route-20261030
```

**Claim first.** Create your output file with these two lines, then push. If the push conflicts or the file exists, take the next item.

```text
lead: <provider>/<model>/<effort> <session or worker name>
lease_until: <now + 12 h, UTC>
```

To share, run `git pull --rebase origin claude/ym4d-hard-route && git push origin HEAD:claude/ym4d-hard-route`. A machine with no push rights to this branch leaves its files for the lead, who commits them.

**Remove the worktree** when your work is pushed, with `lab_ops.py worktree-remove`.



## Working efficiently

Measured on the pushes so far: a resumed push carries about 300,000 to 580,000 tokens of context on every step, and 70 to 90 percent of its active time is the model generating text. A fresh, narrow session avoids most of that: the first fresh check wrote 195,000 tokens for about $8 at API rates, where a resumed push wrote 202,000 for about $219.

- **Hardest first.** Owner, 2026-11-01: go after the hard statements first, to save grinding on the easy base. Work goes to the statement the proof is most likely to die on. A routine hand rebuild, or an elementary citation, waits until a hard-core attempt closes, unless it could be hiding a real gap.
- **One item, one session, run to closure.** Stay on the item until it is proved or refuted, including any smaller statement it reduces to, as long as the budget lasts. Do not move to unrelated items or tidy other files.
- **Do not read whole files.** Part files are 145 to 350 KB, and the folder holds about 1.6 MB of text. Find the statement with `grep -n`, then read the statement, its proof and what it cites.
- **Try to kill it first.** A short computation with pass and fail criteria written first can save a long argument. Report what a run can and cannot establish. A search that finds nothing does not prove a bound.
- **Save as you go.** Write your report section by section. A reader that dies at the output limit with nothing saved is a total loss.
- **Keep reports short.** At most ten defects, ranked by what each breaks. If a defect breaks the consumer's statement, say so and stop unless the rest is independent of it. Give a repair if you see one, marked as unproved.
- **No subagents** unless the item needs a separate reader. Each one is a full extra cost.

## Rules

- **Write only** your output file, `library/` for a new lemma, and your `checks/` folder. Evidence for another file's owner goes in your report, and the lead forwards it.
- **Check reports** open with one provenance line (model, effort, commit, not an audit), then a verdict per statement (ADVANCE, CORRECT-NO-ADVANCE, WRONG), then only the defects that break something, each with its explicit failure. Keep them short; see How we work, item 4.
- **Label honestly**: gap, unchecked, implication, discharged. A step recalled from memory but not proved is a gap. A truthful gap is never penalized.
- **Literature.** See How we work, items 1 and 2. Stop at a bot check, sign-in or paywall; never pay, never commit a PDF, and do not paste long passages.
- **No computer-assisted steps.** See the section of that name in `GOAL.md`. A run can refute or guide, never discharge. If a statement you need rests on a computed value, label it gap and look for a hand proof.
- **Numerics.** One job per machine, under `python3 scripts/lab_ops.py numeric-lock`. Confirm the acquire succeeded before running, and do not pipe it through `tail`. Set BLAS threads to 1, use `nice`, and have the script stop itself above 2 GB RSS. On the Mac mini, start a numeric job only at 50 percent memory free or more, and never use a process pool.
- **Never** edit axioms, audit files or ledgers, run an audit, push to main, or post anything public; the owner approves every public post's exact text.
- **Ask the owner only** about axiom edits, public posts, spending and pace. A scientific fork gets probes.

## Prompt for a new worker

```text
Work on PR #10132 of jonathonreilly/qubit-lattice-axiom-framework, branch claude/ym4d-hard-route. Read .claude/science/physics-loops/ym4d-hard-route-20261030/FINISH.md and GOAL.md in that folder. Take the first free item in the queue, follow its rules, finish that item only, push it, and stop with a short result: verdicts or what was proved, and what is still open.
```
